ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e7620	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Good morning! Christmas history already begins with a census, since Joseph and his pregnant wife have to return to his hometown to register there. The census is something that has existed for many years, decades, millenniums, and at that time, thanks to God, many things have changed. The registration law is a follow-up to the census; in this way we regulate how we regularly raise the population level and certain characteristics. Today, as the first item on the agenda, we have an amendment to the Register Counting Act. The basis for this is a European regulation on population and housing censuses. Finally, 2011 has taken place a register; it is scheduled to take place every ten years, and because many things have changed in these ten years, we decide today on a novel. What has changed everything since 2011? – On the one hand, it is the registers that are used to perform a census. There are new registers, such as the e-health directory, the health profession register, the central register or the Central Citizenship Register. Additional implementing regulations at European level also require the current innovation. But what does that mean? – In 2021, data are used in pseudonymized form, in particular on the basis of the central register, the reporting register, i.e. by means of the sector-specific identification, and certain characteristics on the individual persons such as profession, training, household, company, workplace, but also data on their homes, buildings aggregated in anonymized, pseudonymized version, aggregated and reported to Eurostat. On the one hand, we have a legal obligation under the EU regulation, but this is of course also the basis for a great deal in our country, not least for the communities when it comes to: What people live with us? What does the financing key look like for this area? We have already made it clear in the Committee that we will take a unanimous decision on this. I should like to thank you very much for this and wish us a good way forward for today's debate. Thank you.

Not only with us, but worldwide, this danger is not as far away as you mean. ==References== In January of this year, Europe is only very close to a blackout. It is estimated that such a scenario in Austria would result in a national economic damage of 1.2 to 1.5 billion euros. The challenge is that, after a blackout, the systems cannot easily be driven up again, but the power supply can only be restored gradually. This can take a lot of time. So we have to try everything on the one hand to minimize this danger and at the same time prepare ourselves for the serious situation. The equipment of our police inspections with inselable photovoltaic systems and the necessary battery stores is an essential step in preventing the serious situation. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP as well as by Mr Schalmeiner and Mr Jakob Schwarz.]] In addition, for example, there is a cooperation between the German army and the police to ensure the fuelling of the vehicles in such a blackout. Of course, we also have emergency power units, but our Federal Chancellor made it clear in the summer of this year that this is an obsolete technology. The aim is to provide the police inspections with sustainable and autonomous energy. With this request we meet two demands: On the one hand, we take the necessary steps to prevent in the event of a crisis, and at the same time we do so in line with sustainability and the view to renewable energies. This makes a big contribution to the environment and to climate protection. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] For me, however, it is essential that this step be taken by all parties. Dear spectator at home, you see – as my previous speaker has already said –: It is not only being debated and often disputed here in the House, but very many decisions are taken together. As a policy, we have to deal with new crises over and over again. But it gives me courage to take this important step together in a national shoulder closure. This has already worked very well at the beginning of this crisis. In this sense a warm thank you for the broad approval and also a great thank you to Karl Mahrer for his good Presidency and everything he did in the Security Committee. – A warm thank you!

We are now discussing an amendment to the Administrative Enforcement Act under this item on the agenda, with which the so-called flexibility is reintroduced. This repair was necessary because the Constitutional Court had removed the instrument of flexibility due to various irregularities. [[President Bures shall take over the chairmanship.]] In principle, the legal instrument of bending is useful. It serves to enforce state decisions imposing an obligation on the notifier to act. The main scope of the flexible would be asylum and foreign law. We know: Negative asylum decisions impose an obligation on these persons to leave. You'd have to leave the country. If they do not, then they should be arrested until they are ready to leave. I use the form of opportunity, because we know: That's the theory. There are countless leavers among us, they move freely, they are not illuminated with a bending and can count on all mildness. Now, however, the reintroduction of the flexible with 1.1.2022 is given special brisance, because one month later, on 1st. February 2022, the planned vaccination requirement enters into force. This is also an act to be carried out by the notify recipients, namely the intention of vaccination. However, while those who leave the country can expect or be treated with all the possible mildness, this is of course not the case with the unvaccinated persons according to the previous experience. If so, then suddenly every hardness is attached. It is now stressed – also by Minister of Constitution Edtstadler – that there is no obligation to enforce vaccination. However, this is only right in terms of the replacement penalty – it takes effect if fines for non-fulfilment of vaccination cannot be imposed. What is not said is that this exclusion does not apply to flexibility, which is another legal instrument. That means: Contrary to all the requirements, the possible use of the flexible, i.e. the arrest of an unvaccinated person until vaccination, has not been excluded. However, it could be quite easy to provide for an explicit legal exception in the present draft implementing law as well as in the draft legislation on vaccination. However, this has not been done in both laws in response to different interpretations. It is only mentioned in the Explanatory Notes to the Imprisonment Act that the application of direct command and forced force is excluded. But you can't buy anything. We know that explanations can or should be used in the interpretation of legislative texts, but they are not binding. And also of the fact that Constitutional Minister Edtstadler in the Committee has suggested that people should not be imprisoned, but that they want to be vaccinated, and that there is a broad political consensus that there should not be any flexibility, the unvaccinated people can buy relatively little, because there was also a broad political consensus that there is no need for vaccination. That is, the current status, the application of the flexible in the enforcement of the vaccination obligation is possible. This applies, for example, when the fine is paid several times, but people refuse to take part to leave the vaccine. Then it will take hold – that is my prediction – in a few months, not today, not tomorrow, but possibly in a half year. Since the Federal Government does not do the least to strengthen the health system, but, quite the contrary, to which personnel are especially pressured by the vaccination obligation, it will become ever closer there, and the emergency can then be extended ever further. And then it'll be called: Yes, we don't want this, we never wanted it, the imprisonment of the flexible is really hurting us now, but to protect people, to protect health, in particular to protect the unvaccinated, we must now take you into custody! ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e9260	Madam President. Dear Minister of the Interior! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Seers! My colleague Prince's speech is a good example of how a new story is told by linking two facts and leaving behind a small yet very important detail. It is a story that unfortunately is still wrong and with which the population – by leaving this relevant and essential detail – is simply led astray. What is it about the flexible and the novice of the administrative enforcement law? – The flexible serves to enforce individual acts. Mrs. Prince, I am right in giving you, it is an important point in a rule of law that individual acts – decisions and findings – can also be enforced. If a person has received a notice with which he is personally obliged to perform an action that he himself has to perform, this obligation can be enforced with a flexible – but only if there is such a notice! The flexible does not serve to enforce a general legal obligation, such as a vaccination obligation. Now also comes the essential detail: The draft COVID-19 Imprisonment Act does not provide for a discretionary obligation so that persons with a decision would be required to be vaccinated. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Worm.]] So that means: If there is no notice, there is no flexibility, and therefore this flexibility does not need to be expressly excluded, because it is simply and decisively impossible under our legal order! [[New call for tender of the Abg. Worm.]] It is also not a punishment, and therefore it is also very important to point out that the draft law on vaccination lays down that there will be no prison sentences. [[Abg. Prince: I am happy when it is!]] Again: Without notice, there is no flexible, and the draft COVID-19 Imprisonment Act does not provide a notice, so there is no flexible either. But why do we change the administrative enforcement law at this time at all? – The Constitutional Court removed the flexibility because it could have lasted too long and because there was only a lack of legal protection. That's what we do with this. Now the obsessives of the flexible are being reintroduced. It is limited to a maximum of four weeks in each case and is at least one year in total. The amount of the fine shall be limited to EUR 2 000. Legal protection is extended: There are arrest checks and, most importantly, the proportionality of the flexible must also be examined. Again: A bower does not serve to enforce a general legal obligation, but it is always about the need to enforce concrete individual acts – i.e. decisions – but which are not provided for in the COVID-19 Implication Act. Whoever creates such a connection, who lives in such a world of thought, actually reveals an authoritarian attitude, and I tell you one thing: The ÖVP and this government guarantee that such a world of thought will certainly not be a reality.

In the future CAP, which we are currently in the final negotiations, we will definitely increase the compensatory allowance for mining plants and exploit the possibilities in the field of redistribution. We are planning to support the mining industry in the next agricultural reform. The income of organic farms has increased by 1 percent thanks to God. We have had to take a minus of 10 percent in 2019. It is always tried to include everything possible in the agricultural policy: Social policy issues, how the situation is, this and that – all of which, however, belongs to the social issues. In this area too, you might be happy to support us. What did we hear when it came to the farmer's pensions? – That we're just making client policy. Loacker.]] In order to increase production, we need sustainable development, a sustainable intensification. We need business results with which the enterprises can work accordingly – only those are the successes. ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e12437	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We now decide, among other things, to extend the education bonus, which is very encouraging from a number of points of view, but also an absolutely correct and important step. What is the educational bonus? – With regard to the increased educational bonus in the framework of the Corona work programme, we have decided here in this House that people who take AMS measures in the field of education and qualification or vocational reorientation will receive an increased education bonus of 180 euros per month if these trainings last longer than four months. This is very useful for two reasons. The first is: Very often, people who are affected by unemployment who receive unemployment benefit or emergency aid simply cannot afford longer, more intensive, high-quality education measures economically. That is, they have to stop them earlier, they can't do them at all, they have to go back to jobs that are poorly paid, which are not so stable in the case of which unemployment is more often affected and faster. The second point is that experience shows that the more intensive courses are, the better courses – and unfortunately these are often those that last longer than four months – the more they result in better income, more stable employment and much lower unemployment. That is to say, the extension of this measure is absolutely sensible. It is both sensible and very targeting to deal with two problems: the problem of unemployment on the one hand and the problem of the often cited shortage of skilled workers on the other. What we are going to decide today – and I am particularly pleased, we have already announced it yesterday – is the compensation for people who were in emergency aid or unemployment for at least 30 days in November and December. It is a support amounting to 150 euros to pay off the rise in inflation, the rise in price developments in particular those that do not have great economic ease. And people in unemployment, people who get emergency aid, definitely belong to this group. That is why I would like to see the amendment tabled by Mr August Wöginger, Mr Mag. Markus Kozaand colleagues on the report of the Committee, 1230 of the Supplements, concerning a federal law amending the Unemployment Insurance Act 1977, in which exactly this cost compensation is taken into account. We are increasing the investment programme, but above all we are consistently developing it. This also applies to the four major priorities that we have set ourselves in this framework plan: first of all, the expansion of local transport in the agglomerations. The train is the backbone of its everyday mobility for many commuters, and for countless people it is the guarantee that they come to work on a daily basis and then safely return home. This is, of course, particularly true in the centres of constituency. This is why there is a massive focus in the framework plan. We will expand from the Rhine valley in Vorarlberg to the S-Bahn line in Vienna, improve the infrastructure and make sure that we have enough trains in the future and can travel quickly enough and on time. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The second point is the shift from freight to rail: We have also defined objectives in the Mobility Master Plan. Not only passenger transport, but also freight transport plays a very important role in a climate-friendly transport system of the future. For the first time, this new framework also includes construction measures for the extension of the northern inlet of the Brenner, which will create a four-track high-performance line between Schaftenau and the Knot Radfeld. This creates the basis for more freight traffic on the railway on this important route; one of the most important transport routes in Europe from north to south, where the relocation to the railway is simply the key to solving the local problem. Of course, all Members of the European Parliament here in the Chamber can report it quite differently. As a third priority, we have the electrification: The train runs electrically, all over Austria. Climate protection on the rail means, above all, one thing: less emissions. However, this also includes the conversion of rail transport itself: away from fossil fuels to clean electricity. Even today, 90 percent of the train power on the ÖBB network is electrical. By 2035 it is expected to become 100 percent, so the Framework Plan focuses on the electrification of railway lines, including that throughout Austria. By 2030 we will electrify 500 kilometres of the Austrian railway network, from the Mattigtal Railway in Upper Austria to the Traisental Railway in Lower Austria to the Styria East Railway. Getting There Everywhere Dieselloks are replaced by modern electric trains, operated with green electricity from Austria. All these projects are really an important contribution to a future-fit railway, for a railway that really protects our climate with every ride. Electricalization is one, digitization the other. Digitalization provides in the system Railways for more efficiency, but also make train rides more comfortable. This includes modern, digital information systems on the railway stations as well as a good mobile phone reception on the train – I think I also address a wishful idea quite, quite a lot in this room – and in the process, electronic intercoms help us increase the performance of the rail system. The new ETCS train safety system ensures that we reach the goal not only quickly, but also safely. Thus, digitization and efficiency increase are a very important part of this ÖBB framework plan. These major priorities determine the orientation of the ÖBB Framework Plan 2022 to 2027, but we have also included new projects: In addition to the implementation of the Brennerenordrunschlauf, we are expanding important hubs such as Innsbruck and Villach. We begin with the plans of the two-track extension between Herzogenburg and Sankt Pölten in Lower Austria and between Kirchdorf an der Krems and Micheldorf in Upper Austria. We also plan the speed increase to 120 kilometers per hour in the Arlberg Tunnel. However, the ÖBB Framework Plan is not just a paper, but the framework plan can be seen on new railway lines across the country. I am therefore pleased to be able to report here today that first projects from the last frameworks have already been completed: I was allowed to open the new Karawankenbahn tunnel in summer. We have already completed the electrification of the route between Gänserndorf and Marchegg, and with the current timetable change, the extension of the route around Steindorf in Upper Austria and Salzburg and the two-track extension between Hard and Lauterach in Vorarlberg are being completed. In total, these are many projects for much more climate protection in our country, because especially in the transport sector, we need these, especially in the transport sector, in the fight against the climate crisis enormous opportunities – we also benefit them. That's good news. This also increases the capacity on the train and is very important when connecting if we call Villach or Ried in the Innkreis or also Imst in the Pitztal – that was already called –, Imst and the Imst-Pitztal stop. The rapid implementation of barrier-free railway stations is a very important area within the framework of the ÖBB Etape Plan. Further: Quality measures at the Wiener Stammbahn; the expansion of the Pyhrnachse – these are important examples. The second area, which is particularly important to me as a Tyrolean Member, is, of course, the development of freight transport in order to transfer freight to the railways in order to extend from the railways third of which we have about 40 percent to the modal split by 2040. The third area: The track electrifies, decarbonizes – a very important area. The Mattigtal Railway has already been mentioned; the modernisation of the Radkersburg Railway, electrically between the tent road and Pöls. Digitalization is important. With digitization, there is more security, a higher speed is possible and a better branching. It has already been mentioned: A higher speed, even in the Arlberg tunnel, allows for a better and faster connection of Vorarlberg, better clocking, better utilization with more safety. Today it has also been called the integration of the communities and the integration of the countries, I believe that this is a very essential area, especially when it comes to implementing Park and Ride, implementing noise protection measures, expanding terminals, attacking the mobility hubs and setting up mobility for citizens. We are strengthening rail transport, we are creating 15 000 jobs through investment, we are working on the mobility turnaround. Finally, I would like to say thank you to all those who work outside on the rails every day, working inside the trains that work in the administrative buildings that continue to work on the plans used daily for rail transport: You didn't do it without you. We decide the framework conditions. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e19416	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear listeners at home in front of the screens! Today, the proposed amendment to the Shipping Act provides for the harmonisation of European rules on professional qualifications in inland waterway transport. This creates uniform minimum standards of qualification and mobility for all nautical occupations, which will facilitate mobility in Europe and increase safety. In concrete terms, for example, the physical fitness, the proof and scope of the journey time as well as the standards for tests for ship leaders and sailors. The patents acquired are valid throughout Europe, which is why the mobility of the To facilitate the use of crew members. By means of uniform and high standards of qualification, maritime safety is increased and human life is protected. The improvement of environmental protection – and this has already been done by my colleague, Weratschnig – also ensures qualification standards in the areas of fuel-saving driving, waste management and water protection. ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e19497	Mr President. Minister! The revision of the Railway, Bundesbahn and the Accident Investigation Act – 1168 of the Supplements – was completed on 30 July. Proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. Four infringement procedures have been initiated by the European Commission, two due to incomplete transposition, one because of bad implementation and one because of non-implementation of EU directives. Due to the incomplete implementation of Directive 2016/2370, we are already under threat of an action before the Court of Justice of the European Union. The aim of this amendment is to implement the following points: the possibility of the railway infrastructure undertaking to outsource its functions to other appropriate undertakings or bodies and to monitor the conformity of legislation by the Rail Control Commission; the possibility of the railway infrastructure undertaking, the performance of work and related tasks to other railway undertakings or undertakings controlling and outsourcing railway undertakings; and the monitoring of compliance with the law by the Rail Control Commission. There are still many other points, such as: the possibility of the authority not to impose a transport permit on the conditions for this, and the extension of the Authority's obligation to communicate to the European Commission or to the European Union's railway agency. However, I would like to refer in particular to the so-called Seveso III Directive. This Directive is intended to avoid major industrial accidents in establishments dealing with hazardous substances and to ensure a high level of protection due to special requirements on plant safety at European level. Article 13 of the Directive, which is also the subject of infringement proceedings, is now to be implemented. This article deals with the monitoring of the settlement. In this respect, the following amendments are included in the amendment: the identification of the hazard area of a Seveso operation in the design of a railway project in this hazardous area; Obligation on railway undertakings to take precautions to prevent serious accidents in the case of a Seveso operation as a result of the construction and operation of a railway construction project located in the hazardous area of the Seveso operation. I would like to thank the Transport Committee for the treatment of the novels and hope that we will finally implement the EU directives concerned correctly.

[[Phone call at the SPÖ.]] At the summit of the Heads of State or Government in Austria, he has expressed himself clearly against the use of nuclear power, namely that nuclear power should not be given a green pitcher. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Federal Chancellor Karl Nehammer says in connection with this, there can be no Greenwashing, so no ecological representation of nuclear power as something sustainable. This documents the etiquette between Federal Chancellor, Minister for the Environment and the High House. But this fight has not yet been won, important is the positioning. I would like to mention two aspects which have not yet been mentioned, namely the damage caused and the liability. There are estimates that Chernobyl has caused a damage of about 200 billion dollars, Fukushima, the Super-GAU, 260 billion dollars. The question is: Who's paying for this? The international nuclear liability rules have limits, and only the operator and no other contaminants are liable. Austrian nuclear liability law is much sharper because it says it holds the operators and other contaminants much more. Therefore, Austria also advocates that someone operating a nuclear power plant should be liable for the damage – not the general public or the state – when something happens. This is a very important aspect, because you can see from these sums – 260 billion dollars – that this is a bit whitish. It can't be that the state will stand up for it when something happens. And then the same people explain, nuclear power is not dangerous and does not cause great costs. Now, when the idea of mini-atomic power plants comes up, it's a little bit of an attempt to present it a little messy: It's small and nice and not dangerous. But a small nuclear power plant also needs safety and controls. Especially when I have distributed many small nuclear power plants, the question arises: How do I guarantee security, including military security, and how do I collect the highly radioactive waste? How do I collect him together and where do I deposit him? So many, many questions are unexplained. And even if it might sound nice: It's not nice and it's perhaps even more dangerous than a nuclear power plant is anyway. This makes me the second point, the question of the final storage of nuclear waste. There is a long list of warehouses in Europe and the world, but they all store only weak and medium-sized nuclear waste, for example, from medical care. There is no final storage for high-level waste anywhere in the world. That already shows how uncertain this technology is, because essential things are unclear: highly radioactive waste, not a repository – is it stored somewhere? – That can’t be a solution. In this sense, we are fighting nuclear power. Finally, I can say that we have the potential: In Austria, more than half of the households use wood. Counting the production of wood, heat and electricity from the wood heated in Austria is the value of 39 nuclear power plants. The wood heated in Austria for heat and electricity generation replaces 39 nuclear power plants! Well, if that's not an aspect of putting on renewable energies, wood, wind, photovoltaics? We want this dynamic to move forward. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21126	Thank you, Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Today we are talking about the so-called death law. We must react in Parliament because the Constitutional Court has abolished the ban on aid for suicide and set a deadline until the end of this year. I am forwarding that I personally do not share the opinion of the Constitutional Court, but, of course, accept the decision. It is unpleasant that we have been presented with this draft law very briefly. I assume – and so it was told us – that a longer discussion has gone ahead, but not really in the public, but we have only had a review period of just three weeks for such a delicate topic. At least 139 opinions have been received in these three weeks, but they have not been taken into account at all. This time pressure is, of course, detrimental in view of the socio-political significance of this decision. I recognise as positive that there were efforts by the Ministry of Justice to make a decision as balanced as possible, because there are very conflicting interests. I recognize that. It should also be assessed as positive that this draft law, with a limitation on full-year and decision-making persons and on sick persons, should be submitted It's going to be. However, it is unclear what the term means unhealablely ill. This leaves the decisive doctors a wide scope for interpretation, and that, as we know, is always a burden. It is also an uncertainty factor. It is also positive that there is a mandatory investigation by two doctors before the actual process can occur. However, we would be in favour of one of these doctors coming from the field of psychiatry. The period of twelve weeks between the Enlightenment and the actual suicide seems too short to us. We would also be in favour of further information. But now I come to the real criticisms. It starts with my opinion that the term "death supply" is misunderstanding and should be called a correct statement of suicide. Second criticism, as follows: The “admissible person”, as it is stated in the law, should be called a self-willed person, because not all the deaths – and above all the palliative physicians – or even the least mortals are suicidal. So that's misunderstanding. After all, the § 78 Criminal Code – which was repealed by the Constitutional Court – is now being re-regulated. It is now only stated that one can only be punished if one does so for objectionable reasons, if it is a minor, if the person who has been granted a death permit has not suffered from a corresponding disease or has not been duly informed. The provision of death or, as I would say, the statement of suicide is expressly not provided for in the draft law, and that seems somewhat strange to me, because it was the meaning of the draft law that one implements it. So this is also a criticism point. It is particularly problematic from our point of view that the actual process of self-killing is not regulated in detail. That's an extremely delicate point. It must be imagined that it is not a trained person who assists the person who has died, the willful, but it is someone who has been asked to say yes. But what happens when unexpected side effects occur? What happens if the preparation does not work enough to be fatal? What about the first aid? The person is not allowed to provide any first aid, because he should actually participate in a suicide. What if one might verbally express something that one believes might have to help him now because he gives this impression? So it would have to be clear that first aid measures should not then be put in place. But this mental pressure must be horrendous. There are also no clear rules on the reporting obligations of those involved to clarify that this crime is not then fulfilled. Documentation of the reasons for the assisted suicide and the reporting obligation of implementation would also be important for scientific processing. This is not regulated either. Another point we find very delicate: What happens to the deadly preparation that is spent if it is not used completely or if perhaps this process is not carried out? This is not regulated in the draft law. The Pharmacies Chamber also criticised this in an opinion. After all, it's a deadly preparation that's in circulation. So there's no regulation. In the end, it is not at all foreseen that those persons who participate in the aid, which, as I have already said, are under enormous mental pressure – before the process, during the process and beyond – to be cared for. Who can they turn to? Who do they need to tell? Who Helps In case of doubt – police, official, whoever? They are completely left alone in the situation in which they are exposed to this mental pressure. To sum up, I would like to say: For us, it is crucial that in the future – and this was an essential point, but it was also considered; I recognise the natural – suffering and impaired persons not being pressured to commit an assisted suicide in order not to fall to the burden – that is a very delicate point – and that the aid for self-killing does not become a business model. That's very important to us. What is more important and what would in fact be crucial is that palliative medicine is significantly expanded. This is planned, but unfortunately not really happening. Summary: This draft law leaves so many questions open in such a delicate area that we will not agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21230	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! About a year ago, the Constitutional Court announced a decision by which it repealed the ban on aid for suicide without exception. He said that this exclusive ban was contrary to the right to free self-determination, and he decided that this provision should be applied by 31 December. In 2021, it will be out of power. At the same time, he also said that it was necessary to ensure that this assistance could not be misused. In order to find a good solution to this socially complex and important issue, Justice Minister Alma Zadić has launched a unique process by setting up a dialogue forum. There, numerous experts and representatives of different organizations have been able to exchange very controversial positions in a protected atmosphere. Building on this process, something is really unique throughout Europe: We create the opportunity for people who are dying and for unhealable sick people to be able to end their lives themselves in a dignified way. In the preparation of this law and before, too, I have had many, many conversations with different people from various institutions. Almost every one of these talks has again come up with new aspects, and all these aspects, these different positions, approaches, demands and proposals, have been taken very seriously and included in the drafting of this law. With the law of death, we provide for seriously ill and incurably ill people the opportunity to obtain a preparation that certainly works fatally and that they can take in an environment they choose, as it seems most appropriate for them; for example, at home where the people they love and feel uplifted. At the same time, we also ensure that freedom of conscience is safeguarded, for it is foreseen that no one can be forced to provide assistance and, vice versa, that no one can grow up a disadvantage from the decision he has made – whether he wants to provide the help or not. It is important that there is an informed and self-determined decision which is the basis for this decision. For this, we have provided that there must be two educational talks with different doctors. It must explain exactly where this desire for suicide comes from and what alternative options these persons can be offered. It is only after that that you can complete your death at a notary or notary or at the patient's attorney's office. Such a regulation – and that is very important to me – is nowhere else. It is equally important to me at the same time that it is mentioned that we will also massively increase the resources for the prevention of suicide. Of course, it is good to have this opportunity now, but even better if you can help these people a step ahead. Suicide prevention is a very essential instrument for this. That is why there will be a massive increase in resources for this. But it is just as important to us that the decision can be made freely, and I can only make a decision if I have alternatives. Therefore, it also needs the massive comprehensive expansion of palliative and hospice supplies. This will come and start from the next year. There will be a massive increase in resources so that a decision can actually be made between two alternatives available to both parties. I would like to take the opportunity to thank all those who helped in the legislative process, especially the Minister of Justice, the Minister of Health and the staff involved. In the same way, I thank our staff in the club and all those who have delivered their valuable contributions in conversations in this process, in very long and numerous conversations. Not least, I would like to thank the coalition partner, because I think that this law shows that you can do something good together even if you really want different. What led us to this common result was that from the very beginning we had the same goal: a good regulation in the sense of the people they need. - Thank you.

The difference to the subject of this death law is actually not such a big one. In one case, fate has decided, in the other case, the person concerned decides in a fateful way. I am very grateful to the Constitutional Court for having made a year ago this realization, which takes out the free, self-determined decision from the taboo zone and makes it something that we simply have to accept and where it is right to provide help. The important issue for us is to prevent abuse. If I now measure the present draft law on this scale, I recognise that the attempt to regulate this claim is in line with this claim. Otherwise, the law is not perfect both in terms of formal status – that is what the previous speakers have already done – and in terms of content, in terms of content, and I would not be surprised if it were the subject of the deliberations in the Constitutional Court again. On the other hand, we are dealing with a very sensitive social ethical issue. Against this background, we recognise and will take this first step of regulation. We are convinced, however, that further regulations will then be needed. Finally, I would point out that those countries that have liberal regulations have lower rates of suicide than we do in Austria. That should make us think. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21692	Mrs. Alma Zadić has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21714	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Dear Seers! We've got it. It already belongs: With the recognition of the Constitutional Court of Justice of the 11th century. In December 2020, it was declared unconstitutional that the previous scheme prohibiting aid for suicide was unconstitutional. Thus, as the Federal Government, we faced a huge challenge: Within a year we had to find a regulation for this highly sensitive and also very emotional issue; and yes, that was not a simple matter for all of us, because it was an emotional issue and it had to be made a very difficult decision. It was all the more important for us to find a prudent solution to give seriously ill people a way to end their lives themselves and in dignity at a very difficult time, while also preventing abuse, but also protecting life. The law of death for you is the result of a comprehensive process. We have tried, within the framework of this dialogue forum in the Ministry of Justice, to include, as far as possible, all those concerned by the regulation and to hear their voice in the legislative process, and to give them a vote in this discussion. This dialogue forum was marked by mutual listening and understanding. Representatives were religious representatives, ethics, lawyers, all of whom were heard as well as physicians, representatives of stakeholders and many other organisations concerned. We have also been guided by this in order to make a corresponding arrangement. Our main aim was to implement the recognition of the Constitutional Court responsibly, taking into account, on the one hand, all kinds of wishes and, on the other hand, serious fears. With this government template, we draw a clear path – a clear path that gives legal certainty that protects free will and also ensures the necessary protection from abuse. I would now like to outline the new Mortgage Act shortly: In the future, seriously ill and incurably ill people who are full-year and capable of decision-making are expected to have the possibility of assisted suicide. Of course, the condition is that the person is informed by two doctors and that the disease is detected. The doctors also need to confirm the decision-making capacity. After a period of twelve weeks – in people who are in a terminal phase of their lives due to the disease, this period can also be reduced to two weeks – a so-called death provision can be established for notaries or patient lawyers. This death supply then allows access to a fatal preparation in the pharmacy. Ladies and gentlemen, we are talking about a very sensitive issue that affects us all because it concerns the fundamental question of being human. We all want our loved ones, our family members to do well at the end of their lives, to spend their evening and their end of life in dignity, but also to get the necessary support they need. At the same time, no one should choose the path of dying if there are other possibilities. No one should choose this path because of a personal situation, a financial situation or family affairs. That is why at the same time we are launching a massive expansion of hospice and palliative supplies. The law has already been reviewed and, of course, the Federal Government, as part of this third funding, provides EUR 108 million for countries and social security institutions. At the same time, 2.5 million euros have now been decided in the Council of Ministers on suicide prevention and psychosocial support. In preparing the bill, we were guided by three important criteria: respect for human dignity, respect for life and respect for the self-determined and highly personal decision of seriously ill people. However, I would also like to take this opportunity to thank all those who have contributed to this scheme, especially all participants in the Dialogue Forum and all those who have also come to me personally and who have discussed their concerns and concerns with me. First of all, of course, I would like to thank the coalition partner and the committee chairman, Steinacker, as well as the judicial speaker Agnes Sirkka Prammer, for knowing how difficult it was to find a common arrangement, and I know that both of them were also working around the clock to find the best possible solution. I would also like to thank the President-in-Office and the Judge-General of the other parties, the opposition parties – in the case of Judge Yildirim as well as in the case of Judge-General Margreiter – not only for the preliminary discussions on this regulation, but also for the fact that they and their political groups have supported it in the Committee. I would also like to thank the employees of my house because they were also in use around the clock, so that we can decide this solution and this death law in good time before the end of the year. I would also like to thank all of you for being able to deal with this in good time here in plenary, so that there is no legal uncertainty and no legal gap. I really hope that this government template will receive broad support, and I would like to thank you very much.

[[Abg. Kucharovich: Only if they have their headquarters in Austria! That's the central point!]] As a concrete measure, there is the so-called pre-flaging: Users can already explain when uploading content that it is in the specific case an authorised use, because the content is shared in the form of a quote, criticism or satire. Why are these safety guards important against overblocking? – For example, there have been cases in the United States where police have played copyrighted music during demonstrations. The objective behind this was apparently to make it difficult for demonstrators to distribute demo recordings in social networks, since they were blocked in the background by automated systems due to copyrighted music. It is therefore important to introduce safeguards against the misuse of copyright. Furthermore, small parts of works, i.e. short sections of films or music, are not to be automatically blocked. Thus, neither is the sharing and creation of memes or short videos prohibited. In the event that platforms are systematically putting in place superfluous blocking measures that prohibit authorised use, the competent supervisory authority shall also have the opportunity to initiate a procedure. Finally, I would like to thank all stakeholders and NGOs who have supported and accompanied this process significantly. In my view, we were able to present a very successful and balanced novelle on this very delicate subject. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e23788	Mr President. Estimated Minister of Justice! High house! My predecessor, Mr Bayr, has already said this: This item on the agenda is an EU directive, which provides that the minimum amounts covered by the So-called Minimum Insurance Totals Valorisation Act 2021 are adjusted every five years. In concrete terms, the minimum coverage amounts increase by 2.4 percent by the prescribed index adjustment. In other words, the minimum amount of coverage will be 1.3 million euros per accident victim or a total of 6.45 million euros per injury and 1.3 million euros for property damage. Thus, this index adjustment, without increasing premiums for policyholders, is intended to create a Union-based state in order to comply with the EU Directive. The standard for this is the harmonised consumer price index. This is the basis for a comparable measurement of inflation in Europe and thus monetary stability in the euro area. This is an important parameter, because in the event of material damage, the time value becomes relevant, and inflation increases the time value. Accordingly, insurance amounts need to be adjusted to ensure that they are still sufficiently insured. On the amendment of the SPÖ: Mrs Bayr, you have already announced to the Justice Committee that you want to make that change. You already knew at that time, but you have brought it in too short a time, that is, only yesterday evening. That is why we do not agree today. I may be allowed to explain briefly what the difference is. The original industrialism, as formulated until this change of law, has said: “Compulsory action is committed by a criminal act, which it intends to take on continuously through its recurring approach.” – That was perfectly clear and simple. However, the current legislation states: “Competition is committed by an act which, through its recurring approach for a longer period of time, is not only a minor continuous income” – that is, now must be 400 euros per month converted to the whole year – “using special abilities or means [...] which suggest a recurring journey, or [...] have already planned two other such acts in detail or [...] has already committed two such acts”. – It is also pointed out that the actions will expire within a year. This is therefore completely inapplicable and has led to the fact that the persecution of dealers was no longer possible. In the past, at least arrests could be imposed. It's no longer possible. In particular, a number of police officers, including the President of Vienna, Mr Pürstl, have pointed out that this has caused a security fiasco that the dealers are laughing at the police because they know that the police can't do anything anymore. They then consider whether to make an advertisement at all, because they do not even know if this would lead to a persecution at all. Therefore, we believe that one can admit that one has made a mistake that the reality has shown that the change of law that one has made has led to a negative development. This shows the size and recognition of the important social and social contribution it is by severely restricting drug abuse. It affects mainly young and even often very young people. If we make a meaningful contribution here and give the authorities the opportunity to pursue this really meaningfully and consistently, it is only positive. So I hope you show this size and go with us here.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e24609	Mrs Carina Reiter is now speaking. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e24633	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate viewers! Based on the 2016 criminal law reform, the current legal situation looks such that the possession of small amounts of drugs for own consumption does not automatically lead to an indication when the perpetrators cooperate with the health authorities. Austrian law provides for various alternatives to punishment in the case of drug-using lawbreakers. The pure punishment often does not bring any insight or change in behavior in the case of addictions. That is why the principle of therapy applies before punishment. The causes of the development of addiction problems are not just the availability of psychoactive substances or other offers. It has complex causes and contexts why addiction occurs. Social and health measures may already lead to improvement. It is always about human beings, and therefore it is differentiated in criminal law. In general, the Ultima Ratio always refers to the last means or the last way out. In other words, the respective measure is used in accordance with the principle of proportionality. This application is a direct withdrawal of the 2016 criminal law reform, which was carried out just under the principle of therapy prior to punishment. We therefore reject this request to amend the Criminal Code and the Addiction Act.

Our focus on this novel is quite clearly on: Our houses need to be air conditioned. With the help of this new regulation, we will take another big step in this direction. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The individual measures in detail: First – very important – there is now a right to plug instead of a complicated paperwork. From now on, the installation of e-loading stations or the construction of photovoltaic systems will be much easier. Musts So far, for the installation of a socket, for example in the underground garage, the consent of all co-owners or co-owners, the Right to plug applies from now on. Right to plug means in this sense: One must inform the co-owners of the planned structural measure, let alone it is considered as consent. This simple type of consent applies not only to e-loading stations – I have already mentioned photovoltaic systems – but also very important for conversion measures for accessibility, but also for shade systems in the heat summer. The second one, and I am particularly pleased: We introduce a moderate minimum setback, which in a few years – I am confident to say so – will ensure a reorganisation boost. We were able to observe in the past that in many multi-party homes the renovation failed to ensure that the deposit accounts were not filled enough. These poorly filled deposit accounts then often lead to individual residents saying: That's just not worth this issue. And yes, there are also many individuals who cannot afford several thousand euros simply like that. What does that mean? – Certainly not that living gets more expensive, quite the contrary. The savings are made with a moderate reserve – 90 cents – much more constant over several years. The current system, as it holds very often, is that you start with low or no reserves and then rise extremely after 20 years, has not proved successful. What do we want? – We want to avoid bad cost surprises in the future. I also trust to promise: Our houses will become a good piece of climate, ladies and gentlemen, with the mandatory minimum reserve! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The third greater part of this amendment concerns the voting in the ownership community. These are clearly facilitated: In the future, the decision-making process in ownership communities will focus more on those co-owners who actively participate in the creation of will. In addition, there are improvements in delivery, and owner's meetings can also be held online. Overall, I think we've managed a very, very good package. I would also like to thank the Minister of Justice and, in particular, colleagues Hans Singer for the constructive cooperation. Finally: I therefore have to table an amendment, namely Mr Johann Singer, Mr Nina Tomaselli, colleagues, on the report from the Committee on Buildings and Housing on the Government template (1174, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Act amending the 2002 Housing Property Act (WEG-Novelle 2022 – WEG-Nov 2022) (1286 d.b.) (TOP 39). The application has already been distributed several hours ago. As a matter of principle, the re-establishment of the values of use is as we have always proposed. In the version of the Government template, there are no rules or criteria on how the new adoption should take place. This is what we are doing with this amendment, and of course we are pleased with your consent. Thank you. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] [[20:17]] The application reads as follows:

Of course, each of these reforms has many consequences: Case law needs to focus on the need to write new comments, to print new law books – all this is a huge effort. I would already like to ask the question in the room why one has not dared to take up a larger reform, which is actually overdue. That's what's going on in this As has been said, the government's proposal is partly to be welcomed, namely on climate change measures. However, there are also some regulations that we believe – especially this thing with charging stations – that they are not quite practical – to be placed on slow loading, for example – because we know that the technical development is going to take place very quickly. In this respect, it would be appropriate to provide for a more dynamic system in order to keep pace with progress. What we also see critically is the minimum reserve of 90 cents now laid down in the law. In our view, this does not correspond to the diversity of residential property. As already mentioned, it is about the different conservation status, in particular the different state of climate protection, but also about technical equipment. The maintenance effort is greater if an elevator is inside if other technical facilities or Community facilities are inside. Since we have a value of 90 cents over it, we do not think that it is fair, and that will also bring problems in practice. We would therefore like to see a major reform of the right of home ownership in the future. We are willing to participate in this, and we will agree with it. We do not agree with this work now. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-12-16-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00137_d8e25636	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! Climate change must be combated by all means and at all levels, and we urgently need to fight against this. And yes, an essential factor is, of course, the exit from fossil fuels. This is a very important factor in transport, and therefore the exit from fossil fuels to electric mobility is also an important pillar. However, the changeover to electric vehicles requires that there is a sufficient infrastructure. The possibility and the relief of creating this infrastructure is now being implemented with this novel, the so-called Right to plug is finally realized with this novel. We want to have a future-fit charging infrastructure so that the last obstacles have also been taken when it comes to the changeover to electromobility. On the one hand, it is about the construction of charging devices for electric vehicles in residential property buildings – yes, that will be facilitated – and on the other hand, it is a question of optimizing the general conditions of housing property law for climate-related improvements of buildings, because yes, we also need air-conditioned buildings. In addition, this novelle also implements the long-term requirement that we finally facilitate the creation of will in the ownership community in property law. Ladies and gentlemen. According to previous law, a homeowner may, with the consent of other homeowners, make changes to his home or storage place, for example, install these charging stations. In practice, however, it is extremely difficult to obtain this consent, for example, because the addresses cannot be found or are not known at all or the owners simply do not respond. And we've removed these obstacles. It is now clearly regulated that the administrator or administrator must issue the contact details of the other owners for this purpose. If a homeowner asks for permission to install a charging station, it shall in future be considered as consent if this measure is not contradicted in writing within two months. We have regulated a so-called approval fiction here. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Hörl.]] This is not only about charging stations, but also about many other measures, such as solar installations, shade plants, but also accessibility. I believe that we are taking a step in the right direction with this approval fiction in property law. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In addition, decisions taken by the owner community should be generally facilitated. How do we do that? – So far, it has been the case that every decision always requires half of all owners. That's what we're changing now. It is now up to a qualified majority of the votes cast. There are accompanying measures. In the review it has been said time and again that it can come to abuse from a very small active co-ownership group. – No, we can’t, because we have flanking measures. On the one hand, a two-thirds majority of the votes cast are required, and on the other hand, one third of all co-ownerships have to take decisions. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that with this innovation, we are taking a very important step towards emissions-free transport and are implementing a climate-friendly housing together. I would also like to thank the Minister for Climate Change, with whom I have taken these steps in this context, and of course also the Coalition Partner. I hope you will agree.

Finally to our own request, that of the Freedom Party: What is the point of this proposal? – Women who opt for the income-dependent child care allowance and in the period before the child is born more than 14 days of sick leave, lose their entitlement to it or have financial losses, and these are significant. Often, child care benefits are reduced by: Half. An example of this: For example, a mother concerned expected a daily amount of 66 euros and received only 33 euros because she had received more than 14 days of sickness benefit. This corresponds to a loss of almost 12 000 euros per year. The People's Procuratorate has also made an alarm and has proposed that a legal change be made in order to avoid cases of hardship. Those periods of sickness benefit are to be included under the term "employment" in the income-related childcare allowance – this is also handled by the EU, as sickness benefit is to be seen as a cash benefit and is to be regarded as an employment exercise. This is the EU's view, and I therefore urge the government parties to take the public prosecutor's recommendation seriously and to reconsider the rejection of our request. Thank you.

The three-part legislative package is structured as follows: The first is the development of the family allowance procedure – Fabian says – and the second is the extension of the family allowance after graduation and the third is the increase in the amount of child care allowance. The development of the family aid procedure is therefore so important because it is the central procedure for the distribution of family aid. It has been in operation since March 2021, and we are trying, as I said, to constantly evaluate and improve it. We now want to relieve families and, in particular, students of bureaucracy by providing an automatic extension of the family allowance if all the requirements of entitlement are met. This means no more paper documents for the families, no more official channels, because the student data of the universities are automatically forwarded and can be processed. Secondly, as has been said, it is about extending family aid after completion of schooling. We want the family allowance to be automatically continued four months after completion of schooling. This enables us to ensure two things: on the one hand, that there is no gap between education and any start of studies, and on the other hand, that all those who start to work after graduation are paid on these four months, just as a reward for the successful completion of schooling. And thirdly, we raise the amount of child care allowance, from 7 300 to 7 600 euros. This is important, therefore, because one can work in the same way, can be slightly busy and thus, as has been said, can also deserve something to do with it. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to thank you very much. This plenary week has brought much relief to families. I am very happy and proud that we can increase the family bonus to EUR 2 000 for the families, I am very happy and proud that we can increase the amount of children for all those who cannot benefit from the family bonus to EUR 450, and I am very glad that we can also achieve further simplifications, debureaucratisations and the reduction of administrative barriers for the families.
